    },
    "minimumVersion": {
      "description": "Minimum version to stay on - prevents downgrades when switching to stable channel",
      "type": "string"
    },
    "plansDirectory": {
      "description": "Custom directory for plan files, relative to project root. If not set, defaults to ~/.claude/plans/",
      "type": "string"
    },
    "tui": {
      "description": "Terminal UI renderer. \"fullscreen\" uses the flicker-free alt-screen renderer with virtualized scrollback (equivalent to CLAUDE_CODE_NO_FLICKER=1). \"default\" uses the classic main-screen renderer.",
      "type": "string",
      "enum": [
        "default",
        "fullscreen"
      ]
    },
    "voice": {
      "description": "Voice mode settings (hold-to-talk / tap-to-toggle dictation)",
      "type": "object",
      "properties": {
        "enabled": {
          "type": "boolean"
        },
        "mode": {
          "description": "'hold' (default): hold to talk. 'tap': tap to start, tap to stop+submit.",
          "type": "string",
          "enum": [
            "hold",
            "tap"
          ]
        },
        "autoSubmit": {
          "description": "Submit the prompt when hold-to-talk is released (hold mode only)",
          "type": "boolean"
        }
      }
    },
    "channelsEnabled": {
      "description": "Teams/Enterprise opt-in for channel notifications (MCP servers with the claude/channel capability pushing inbound messages). Default off. Set true to allow; users then select servers via --channels.",
      "type": "boolean"
    },
    "allowedChannelPlugins": {
      "description": "Teams/Enterprise allowlist of channel plugins. When set, replaces the default Anthropic allowlist — admins decide which plugins may push inbound messages. Undefined falls back to the default. Requires channelsEnabled: true.",
      "type": "array",
      "items": {
        "type": "object",
        "properties": {
          "marketplace": {
            "type": "string"
          },
          "plugin": {
            "type": "string"
          }
        },
        "required": [
          "marketplace",
          "plugin"
        ]
      }
    },
    "prefersReducedMotion": {
      "description": "Reduce or disable animations for accessibility (spinner shimmer, flash effects, etc.)",
      "type": "boolean"
    },
    "autoMemoryEnabled": {
      "description": "Enable auto-memory for this project. When false, Claude will not read from or write to the auto-memory directory.",
      "type": "boolean"
    },
    "autoMemoryDirectory": {
      "description": "Custom directory path for auto-memory storage. Supports ~/ prefix for home directory expansion. Ignored if set in projectSettings (checked-in .claude/settings.json) for security. When unset, defaults to ~/.claude/projects/<sanitized-cwd>/memory/.",
      "type": "string"
    },
    "autoDreamEnabled": {
      "description": "Enable background memory consolidation (auto-dream). When set, overrides the server-side default.",
      "type": "boolean"
    },
    "showThinkingSummaries": {
      "description": "Show thinking summaries in the transcript view (ctrl+o). Default: false.",
      "type": "boolean"
    },
    "skipDangerousModePermissionPrompt": {
      "description": "Whether the user has accepted the bypass permissions mode dialog",
      "type": "boolean"
    },
    "disableAutoMode": {
      "description": "Disable auto mode",
      "type": "string",
      "enum": [
        "disable"
      ]
    },
    "sshConfigs": {
      "description": "SSH connection configurations for remote environments. Typically set in managed settings by enterprise administrators to pre-configure SSH connections for team members.",
      "type": "array",
      "items": {
        "type": "object",
        "properties": {
          "id": {
            "description": "Unique identifier for this SSH config. Used to match configs across settings sources.",
            "type": "string"
          },
          "name": {
            "description": "Display name for the SSH connection",
            "type": "string"
          },
          "sshHost": {
            "description": "SSH host in format \"user@hostname\" or \"hostname\", or a host alias from ~/.ssh/config",
            "type": "string"
          },
          "sshPort": {
            "description": "SSH port (default: 22)",
            "type": "integer",
            "minimum": -9007199254740991,
            "maximum": 9007199254740991
          },
          "sshIdentityFile": {
            "description": "Path to SSH identity file (private key)",
            "type": "string"
          },
          "startDirectory": {
            "description": "Default working directory on the remote host. Supports tilde expansion (e.g. ~/projects). If not specified, defaults to the remote user home directory. Can be overridden by the [dir] positional argument in `claude ssh <config> [dir]`.",
            "type": "string"
          }
        },
        "required": [
          "id",
          "name",
          "sshHost"
        ]
      }
    },
    "claudeMdExcludes": {
      "description": "Glob patterns or absolute paths of CLAUDE.md files to exclude from loading. Patterns are matched against absolute file paths using picomatch. Only applies to User, Project, and Local memory types (Managed/policy files cannot be excluded). Examples: \"/home/user/monorepo/CLAUDE.md\", \"**/code/CLAUDE.md\", \"**/some-dir/.claude/rules/**\"",
      "type": "array",
      "items": {
        "type": "string"
      }
    },
    "pluginTrustMessage": {
      "description": "Custom message to append to the plugin trust warning shown before installation. Only read from policy settings (managed-settings.json / MDM). Useful for enterprise administrators to add organization-specific context (e.g., \"All plugins from our internal marketplace are vetted and approved.\").",
      "type": "string"
    },
    "theme": {
      "description": "Color theme for the UI",
      "anyOf": [
        {
          "type": "string",
          "enum": [

            "light-daltonized",

        {
          "type": "string",

🏆 Tier 1: Absolute Must-Haves (Install These First)
These are the highest ROI skills, used by almost everyone:

1. 🧠 Superpowers — obra/superpowers
The single most important skill in the ecosystem. Transforms Claude from a "doer" into a project manager — it runs brainstorm → spec → plan → subagent execution → review → merge before anything gets built. Includes 20+ battle-tested sub-skills for planning, debugging, and TDD. Think of it as hiring a senior consultant instead of a junior developer.

github.com/obra/superpowers

2. 📄 PDF Processing — anthropics/pdf
The highest-utility skill for knowledge workers bar none. A 48-page contract that took 90 minutes to review manually takes under a minute — it handles text extraction, table parsing, form filling, merging, and annotation.

github.com/anthropics/skills/tree/main/skills/pdf

3. 🔧 Skill Creator — anthropics/skill-creator
The meta-skill — a skill that builds other skills. You describe your workflow in natural language, answer 3–4 clarifying questions, and get a complete SKILL.md in ~5 minutes. This is how you convert your own domain expertise into a permanent Claude capability.

github.com/anthropics/skills/tree/main/skills/skill-creator

🥈 Tier 2: Power Skills by Domain
4. 📊 Excel / XLSX — anthropics/xlsx
Formula creation, data analysis, chart generation via natural language. Describe a dataset, request regional analysis, and get an Excel file with embedded charts — the barrier between "having data" and "understanding data" drops instantly.

5. 📑 PowerPoint — anthropics/pptx
Generates complete structured slide decks with layouts, charts, and speaker notes from a plain text prompt in ~20 minutes. Eliminates the blank-slide paralysis.

6. 🌐 Google News API Skill — browser-act/skills
Real-time Google News scraping without CAPTCHA blocks. Set a keyword, get a structured feed of current industry coverage on demand — replaces manual RSS monitoring for research and competitive intel.

7. 🛒 Amazon Competitor Analyzer — browser-act/skills
Extracts competitor pricing, ratings, reviews, and market positioning from Amazon automatically. Turns weekly manual competitor checks into a single prompt.

8. 📈 Marketing Skills Bundle — coreyhaines31/marketingskills
20+ marketing skills in one install — CRO, copywriting, SEO, A/B testing frameworks, email sequences, and growth strategy. Built by Corey Haines for founders and technical marketers.

9. 🔍 Claude SEO — AgriciDaniel/claude-seo
Full-site SEO audit: page-level analysis, schema validation, sitemap generation, AI search optimization — using 12 sub-skills with 6 parallel subagents. Replaces multiple manual auditing tools in one run.

10. 🎨 Frontend Design — anthropics/frontend-design
Eliminates AI slop from generated UIs. Embeds a full design system that produces screenshot-shareable dashboards and interfaces — the single biggest quality jump for app builders.

🥉 Tier 3: Research, Knowledge & Intelligence
11. 📓 NotebookLM Integration — PleasePrompto/notebooklm-skill
Bridges Claude with Google NotebookLM. A 30-page technical paper that used to take 3–4 hours now produces a summary, concept mind map, 20-card flashcard set, and research notes in 15 minutes.

12. 🗂️ Obsidian Skills — kepano/obsidian-skills
Built by Obsidian's own CEO — creates properly structured Markdown, auto-tags, auto-links, and generates summaries inside your Obsidian vault, turning it into an AI-augmented knowledge system.

13. 📰 Content Research Writer
Autonomously adds citations, enhances quality, and organizes research — invaluable for any content backed by data or academic sources.

14. 🎥 YouTube Transcript / Article Extractor
Summarizes YouTube videos and web articles for rapid research — cutting hours of manual watching/reading into structured notes in minutes.

15. 📚 EPUB & PDF Analyzer
Queries and summarizes eBooks and academic papers — heavily used by researchers and students. Ask it any question about a 300-page book and get an answer.

⚡ Tier 4: Workflow Automation & Productivity
16. 🏢 OpenClaw Official 53-Skill Bundle — openclaw/openclaw
Production-tested skills covering Obsidian notes, Google Workspace (Gmail/Calendar), GitHub, Slack, CI/CD monitoring, social media, and smart home — all in one install. These are the reference implementations of what great skill architecture looks like.

17. 🤝 Document Co-Authoring — anthropics/doc-coauthoring
Real back-and-forth collaborative writing — human writes a section, Claude responds, alternating. Produces better strategy docs and proposals than either solo human or solo AI writing.

18. 🛠️ Web Artifacts Builder — anthropics/web-artifacts-builder
Builds lightweight web tools — calculators, converters, internal dashboards — via natural language. No frontend knowledge required.

19. 🎨 Brand Guidelines — anthropics/brand-guidelines
Encodes your brand's tone, typography, colors, and formatting once — applies them automatically to every Claude output forever. No more off-brand content.

20. 🐛 Systematic Debugging Skill — via obra/superpowers

# SECTION 2: INDIA NUCLEAR ENERGY — 100GW DEEP RESEARCH

---

## 🏗️ THE BIG PICTURE: INDIA'S NUCLEAR ROADMAP

### Current State (April 2026)
- **Installed nuclear capacity:** ~7,480 MW (22 operating reactors across 7 stations)
- **Share of total power:** ~3% of India's electricity mix
- **Target:** **100 GW by 2047** — a **13x expansion** from current capacity
- **Investment required:** ~₹10–15 lakh crore ($120–180 billion) over 21 years

### Operating Stations
| Station | Location | Capacity |
|---------|---------|---------|
| TAPS (1-4) | Tarapur, MH | 1,400 MW |
| RAPS (1-8) | Rawatbhata, RJ | 1,180 MW + 700 MW (RAPP-7 operating) |
| MAPS (1-2) | Kalpakkam, TN | 440 MW |
| KGS (1-4) | Kaiga, KA | 880 MW |
| NAPS (1-2) | Narora, UP | 440 MW |
| KAPS (1-4) | Kakrapar, GJ | 880 MW |
| KKNPS (1-2+) | Kudankulam, TN | 2,000+ MW |

### Under Construction (NPCIL)
| Project | Capacity | Notes |
|---------|---------|-------|
| KKNPP 3 & 4 | 2,000 MW | Kudankulam, Russian design |
| KKNPP 5 & 6 | 2,000 MW | Advanced stage planning |
| GHAVP 1 & 2 | 1,400 MW | Gorakhpur, Haryana |
| Kaiga 5 & 6 | 1,400 MW | **Construction commenced 2026** |

---

## ⚡ THE ATOMIC ENERGY AMENDMENT BILL ("SHANTI BILL")

### Key Policy Development
India's **Atomic Energy (Amendment) Bill** — informally called the **Shanti Bill** — is the **single biggest catalyst** for nuclear stocks:

- **What it does:** Allows **private sector companies** to enter nuclear power generation (previously a pure government monopoly)
- **Status (April 2026):** Passed/cleared in multiple states; **16 states have endorsed** supporting resolutions
- **Who benefits:** Tata Power, Adani Green, NTPC, JSW Energy — any large private power company can now bid for nuclear plants

### SMR (Small Modular Reactor) Program
| Parameter | Details |
|-----------|---------|
| Designer | **BARC (Bhabha Atomic Research Centre)** |
| Reactor Type | Compact PHWRs — 220 MW scale SMRs |
| Current Status | **3 SMRs in design/development at BARC** |
| Target: Operational | **5 SMRs by 2033** |
| Fuel | Thorium/Uranium — India has world's 3rd largest thorium reserves |
| Advantage | Lower cost, faster deployment, modular scaling |

### Why SMRs Are Transformational
- Each 220 MW SMR costs ~₹5,000–8,000 Cr vs ₹20,000+ Cr for conventional 1,000 MW reactor
- Can be deployed at industrial sites, replacing coal captive plants
- Perfect for state governments wanting low-carbon baseload power
- **Direct demand from:** Tata Power, JSW, Hindalco, Adani (large power consumers)

---

## 🏭 NUCLEAR COMPONENT SUPPLY CHAIN — MARKET SIZE

| Item | Cost per Plant | Suppliers |
|------|---------------|---------|
| Reactor pressure vessel | ₹800–1,200 Cr | L&T, BHEL |
| Steam generators | ₹400–600 Cr | BHEL, Walchand |
| Turbines & generators | ₹600–900 Cr | BHEL |
| Precision components | ₹200–400 Cr | MTAR, Walchand |
| Instrumentation | ₹100–200 Cr | MTAR, Data Patterns |
| Civil construction | ₹2,000–4,000 Cr | L&T |
| **Total per 700 MW reactor** | **₹8,000–15,000 Cr** | |

**If India adds 90+ GW (90,000 MW) by 2047:**
- ≈ 120–130 new reactors (mix of 700 MW PHWRs + SMRs)
- **Total nuclear capex: ₹10–15 lakh crore**
- **Component supply market: ₹2–4 lakh crore** over 21 years

---

## 📈 NUCLEAR STOCKS — DETAILED ANALYSIS & SCORES

---

## PART 2: INDIA NUCLEAR — TARGETS &amp; POLICY

### 2.1 Capacity Roadmap

| Target | Capacity | Announced By |
|--------|----------|--------------|
| **Near-term target** | **22.5 GWe by 2031–32** | DAE, reaffirmed February 2025 |
| **Long-term national target** | **100 GWe by 2047** | Union Budget FY2025-26 (Finance Minister Nirmala Sitharaman) |
| Abandoned previous target | 63 GWe by 2032 | — |

&gt; **Gap Analysis:** From current ~8.88 GW to 100 GW = **~91 GW net addition needed by 2047** (21 years). This implies building **~4.3 GW per year** on average — roughly 5-6x India's historical build rate.

---

### 2.3 SHANTI Bill — Legislative Revolution

**What it is:** The **SHANTI (Strategic, Harmonised, and Accelerated Nuclear Technology Initiative) Bill**, passed **December 2025**, replaces two key laws:
- The **Atomic Energy Act of 1962** (state monopoly on nuclear)
- The **Civil Liability for Nuclear Damage Act, 2010** (which had blocked foreign vendors due to operator liability incompatibility)

**Key Provisions:**
- Private companies can now **"build, own, operate, and decommission"** nuclear power plants
- **Limited private participation under regulatory oversight** (not fully open market)
- Resolves long-standing barrier for **foreign vendors** (Westinghouse, EDF/Framatome, Rosatom)
- **6 private conglomerates** already responded to NPCIL's Request for Proposal (December 2024): **Hindalco, Jindal Steel, Tata Power, Reliance, JSW, Adani Power**
- **16 prospective sites** identified across **6 states**

&gt; ⚡ **This is the single biggest structural change in India's nuclear history.** Opening to private capital and foreign technology is the prerequisite for the 100 GW target being achievable.

## PART 4: FUEL SUPPLY — URANIUM &amp; THORIUM

| Fuel | Data |
|------|------|
| **Domestic uranium identified** | 292,867 tU (as of Jan 2021 Red Book) |
| **Domestic uranium production** | Peaked at 1,062 tU (2015); has fluctuated significantly |
| **Import dependence** | ~40% imported (as of 2013); suppliers: Russia (TVEL), Kazakhstan (Kazatomprom), Canada (Cameco), Uzbekistan (NMMC), Australia |
| **Thorium reserves** | ~319,000 tonnes confirmed; up to 12 million tonnes monazite (AMD estimate) |
| **World thorium share** | ~13% of global reserves |
| **Stage 3 (Thorium cycle)** | Expected "beyond 2070" — long-term strategic goal |

**Three-Stage Nuclear Cycle:**
1. **Stage 1:** PHWRs on natural uranium → produces plutonium
2. **Stage 2:** Fast Breeder Reactors (FBRs) burning plutonium + thorium blanket → breeds U-233 + more Pu *(PFBR now critical)*
3. **Stage 3:** AHWRs on thorium + Pu/U-233 → self-sustaining thorium cycle *(post-2070)*

| Reactor Type | Cost per MW |

| VVER-1000 (Kudankulam 3&amp;4) | **₹20 crore/MW (~$3.1M/MW)** |
| VVER-1000 (Kudankulam 5&amp;6) | **₹25 crore/MW (~$3.85M/MW)** |
| PHWR-700 (typical Indian) | ~₹15–18 crore/MW (est.) |

**Derived Market Size Estimates:**

| Scenario | Capacity Added | $/MW Cost | Total Investment | Component Share (~30-35%) |
|----------|---------------|-----------|-----------------|--------------------------|
| Near-term (to 2031–32) | ~13.6 GW | $3–3.5M | ~$41–48B | **~$12–17B** |
| Full 100 GW by 2047 | ~91 GW net | $3–4M | ~$273–364B | **~$82–127B** |
| GlobalData CAGR | 6.2 GW (2022 base) | — | — | 11%+ CAGR through 2035 |

&gt; 📌 The **domestic component market** (steel, forgings, turbines, steam generators, precision components) typically represents 30–40% of total plant cost in India (higher indigenisation than global average due to PHWR technology being fully domesticated).

## PART 7: MANAGEMENT COMMENTARY &amp; INVESTMENT SIGNALS

### What India's Private Sector Is Saying
- **6 major conglomerates** (Hindalco, Jindal Steel, Tata Power, Reliance, JSW, Adani) formally responded to NPCIL's nuclear plant RFP in December 2024 — **unprecedented in Indian nuclear history**
- These groups are negotiating to **build, own, and operate** nuclear plants (new under SHANTI Bill)
- Each of these groups will need Indian-made components from L&amp;T, BHEL, MTAR, WIL

### BHEL Management Position
- "Only company associated with all three stages of India's Nuclear Power Programme"
- FY2026 order book reported at **~₹2.4 lakh crore** (highest-ever); nuclear portion not broken out
- 2023 NPCIL MoU positions BHEL as preferred partner for new PHWR fleet

### L&amp;T's Structural Advantage
- Created LTSHF JV with NPCIL precisely to build nuclear-grade forgings domestically
- 120 MT forging capacity puts L&amp;T in a class of 5-6 global suppliers capable of reactor pressure vessel forgings

---

| **MTAR Technologies** | ~₹3,000-4,000 crore | ~₹500-600 crore | Est. 20-30% nuclear | Moderate but critical precision parts |
| **Walchandnagar Industries** | Small-cap | ~₹500-700 crore | Historical supplier | Moderate |

---

## PART 9: KEY RISK FACTORS &amp; CHALLENGES

| Risk | Details |
|------|---------|
| **Build Rate Gap** | Historical pace: ~0.5 GW/year; 100 GW by 2047 needs ~4.3 GW/year — 8–9x acceleration |
| **Land Acquisition** | Each plant site requires significant land; social opposition at Jaitapur, Kovvada |
| **Uranium Supply** | ~40% import dependent; need more long-term fuel supply agreements |
| **Skilled Workforce** | Massive shortfall in nuclear engineers, welders, quality control inspectors |
| **Financing** | Nuclear plants are capital-intensive; ₹20,000 crore SMR budget is small vs. need |
| **Regulatory Capacity** | AERB (Atomic Energy Regulatory Board) needs major expansion |
| **Private Sector Learning** | Even with SHANTI Bill, Tata/Adani etc. are new to nuclear — steep learning curve |
| **HALEU Fuel** | Global SMRs face HALEU enrichment bottlenecks (not India-specific for current PHWRs) |
| **Cost Overruns** | PFBR cost went from ₹3,500 cr to ₹8,181 crore (2.3x); global norm is nuclear cost escalation |

---

## PART 10: SYNTHESIS &amp; INVESTMENT IMPLICATIONS

### Bull Case for Indian Nuclear Component Makers
1. **SHANTI Bill (Dec 2025)** = structural demand unlock; private sector now legally able to build plants → **10x increase in potential buyers** for components
2. **₹20,000 crore SMR budget** = guaranteed near-term order flow for BARC-designed SMR components by 2033
3. **100 GW target** = if India achieves even 50% → ~$130-180B total investment → **$40-65B component market** over 20 years
4. **PHWRs are domestically designed** → Indian manufacturers get **entire component supply chain** (unlike imported reactors)
5. **PFBR commercial operation** triggers orders for 2 more FBRs at Kalpakkam

### Key Beneficiaries (Ranked by Nuclear Revenue Sensitivity)
1. 🥇 **BHEL** — Broadest nuclear exposure, all 3 stages, NPCIL MoU, turbogenerators for all plants
2. 🥈 **L&amp;T** (Heavy Engineering + LTSHF JV) — Premier fabricator of large forgings &amp; steam generators
3. 🥉 **MTAR Technologies** — Precision components; smallest but highest nuclear revenue % purity
4. **Walchandnagar Industries** — Historical supplier; watch for re-emergence with order flows
5. **New entrants**: Jindal Stainless (nuclear-grade SS), Mishra Dhatu Nigam (MIDHANI) for specialty alloys

### Bear Case
- 100 GW by 2047 is **almost certainly aspirational** — even France at its nuclear peak built ~3-4 GW/year
- SHANTI Bill implementation will face legal, regulatory, and operational challenges
- Private sector will likely focus on co-investment + power purchase, not component manufacturing initially
- Global SMR delays (NuScale, X-energy) show technology risk is real

---

## ⚡ THE SHANTI BILL — GAME CHANGER

> *Formally: The Atomic Energy (Amendment) Act + Civil Liability Amendment, passed December 2025*

| What Changed | Before | After |
|-------------|--------|-------|
| Who can build nuclear? | Only NPCIL (govt monopoly) | **Private sector + JVs allowed** |
| Foreign vendor liability | Operator + supplier liable (blocked US/French vendors) | **Liability capped, bankable** |
| Private companies | Banned | **6 groups already in RFP process** |
| SMR deployment | Only NPCIL | **Private industrial groups can deploy SMRs** |
| Speed of deployment | Slow (bureaucratic) | Faster (market incentives) |

**The 6 Private Groups in NPCIL RFP (Dec 2024):**
1. **Tata Power**
2. **Reliance Industries**
3. **Adani Power**
4. **JSW Energy**
5. **Hindalco**
6. **Jindal Steel & Power**

> 🔥 This is unprecedented. For the first time in Indian nuclear history, private capital is formally invited. Each of these groups will need **Indian-made components** — directly benefiting L&T, BHEL, MTAR, Walchand.

---

## ⚛️ SMR (SMALL MODULAR REACTOR) PROGRAMME — BARC

| SMR Design | Power | Type | Status |
|-----------|-------|------|--------|
| **BSMR-200** (Bharat SMR) | **200 MWe** | Based on proven PHWR tech | Design phase |
| **SMR-55** | **55 MWe** | PWR variant | Design phase |
| **HTGR** | 5 MWt | High-temp gas (hydrogen) | R&D |
| **BSR** | 220 MWe | PHWR derivative | Conceptual |

**SMR Programme Targets:**
- **₹20,000 crore** government budget allocated (Union Budget FY2025-26)
- **3 SMR designs at BARC** in development
- **5 SMRs operational by 2033** — confirmed government target
- First SMR construction contract expected: **2027–2028**
- Sites: Tarapur already listed (BSMR-200 + SMR-55)

**Why SMRs are the critical stock catalyst:**
- Each SMR = ~₹1,500–2,500 Cr in equipment orders
- 5 SMRs by 2033 = ₹7,500–12,500 Cr in near-term orders
- **50 SMRs by 2047** (if programme scales) = ₹75,000–1,25,000 Cr component market
- Components are 90%+ domestic (same PHWR supply chain)

---

**For 100GW target (130+ reactors):**
- Total nuclear capex: ₹10–15 lakh crore
- Indian domestic component share: **₹3–5 lakh crore**
- This is a 20-year multi-decade order pipeline

**Nuclear Products:** Fuel machining equipment, coolant channel components, shut-off rod mechanisms, reactor internals, precision forgings.

**The Bull Case:** MTAR is one of **3–4 companies globally** certified to make precision nuclear components to NPCIL specs. As India goes from 8.88 GW → 100 GW, MTAR's addressable order book grows 10x. Revenue could scale from ₹750 Cr → ₹5,000+ Cr by 2035.

**The Bear Case:** At 242x P/E, all good news is priced in. Promoter selling aggressively. Profits lagging revenue growth (margins compressed). Stock already 275% up in 1 year.

**Verdict:** Buy on dips below ₹3,500. Core nuclear holding for 5+ years.

### ⭐⭐⭐ WALCHAND INDUSTRIES — Score: 78/100
**The Overlooked Nuclear Heavy Engineer**

| Metric | Estimated (small-cap, limited public data) |
|--------|-----|
| Market Cap | ~₹1,500–2,500 Cr |
| Nuclear Products | Calandria, end shields, heat exchangers, coolant channel fittings |
| Nuclear clients | NPCIL (direct), BHEL (subcontracts) |
| Heritage | 80+ year history of nuclear supply |
| Status | Post-US sanctions (1998–2001), now fully cleared |

**Why it's special:** Walchand makes the **calandria** — the reactor's core structural vessel that holds the fuel channels. This is arguably the single most critical precision component in a PHWR. Very few companies worldwide can make it to nuclear grade. Walchand is one.

**The asymmetric bet:** With a ~₹2,000 Cr market cap and nuclear component demand potentially worth ₹500–1,000 Cr/year to Walchand by 2030, the stock could 5–10x over 7 years.

**Risk:** Small-cap liquidity, limited public disclosures, management opacity.

**Why BHEL is unique:** It is the **only** Indian company supplying all three stages of India's nuclear programme (PHWRs → FBRs → AHWRs). When nuclear builds 10x, BHEL turbine-generator orders alone scale to ₹50,000+ Cr over 20 years.

**The concern:** ROE of just 2.1% is very poor. The company is still in turnaround mode from 2017–2022 slump. At 144x P/E, it's priced for perfection.

**Verdict:** Nuclear theme stock. Buy on corrections. The ₹2.4 lakh crore order book (highest ever) is the signal — execution will follow.

| **X-energy (Xe-100)** | USA | 🟡 Private | 76 | SPAC failed; 2 projects (Dow, Energy NW) |
| **BSMR-200** | **India** | 🟡 Design phase | **200** | **Indigenous; 2033 target; ₹20,000 Cr budget** |

> 🔑 **Key insight:** India's BSMR-200 is actually **LARGER** than most US SMR designs and is based on **proven PHWR technology** — India has 25 years of operating PHWRs. This gives India's SMR programme a technology maturity advantage over US startups.

## ⚠️ KEY RISKS

| Risk | Severity | Mitigation |
|------|---------|-----------|
| 100 GW is aspirational — actual delivery may be 30–50 GW | High | Even 30 GW = 3x current = massive orders |
| Land acquisition delays (Jaitapur, Kovvada) | Medium | PHWRs on existing sites avoid this |
| Uranium supply constraints | Medium | India diversifying to 6+ countries |
| Cost overruns (PFBR went 2.3x over budget) | High | Budget conservatively |
| Private sector nuclear learning curve | Medium | 10+ year runway to build expertise |
| MTAR promoter stake decline (50% → 30%) | High | Watch for further dilution |
| BHEL ROE at 2% — poor capital allocation | Medium | Order book growth solves this |

> **If you can only buy 3 nuclear stocks:**

1. **MTAR Technologies** — The only listed pure-play precision nuclear component maker. Every reactor India builds means more orders for MTAR. 5–10x in 7 years if nuclear executes.

2. **BHEL** — Supplies turbine-generators to every nuclear plant. All three nuclear stages. ₹2.4 lakh crore order book (record). Turnaround + nuclear = double catalyst.

---

# 🔬 COMPREHENSIVE INDIAN NUCLEAR SUPPLY CHAIN RESEARCH REPORT
## Mechanical Components for Nuclear Power Plants — Listed Indian Companies
### Research Date: April 24, 2026

---

## ⚠️ CRITICAL RESEARCH METHODOLOGY NOTES

Before the data tables, three critical caveats must be stated:

1. **NPCIL Vendor List is NOT public.** Despite extensive search, NPCIL's approved vendor list is **not publicly accessible**. The NPCIL orders page (orders &gt;₹10 Cr) links only to PDFs — not scraped by web tools. Nuclear vendor qualification is governed by AERB and DAE, and supplier names are **never disclosed in annual reports** due to national security protocols.

2. **No Indian listed company explicitly declares nuclear revenue %** in their public filings. Nuclear exposure must be inferred from product capability, industry mentions, and supply chain intelligence from company websites and investor presentations.

3. **Screener.in blocked most direct ticker URL attempts.** Data was obtained through BSE ISIN codes and alternate URL structures where direct tickers failed.

---

## SECTION 1: 🔵 PUMPS — NUCLEAR COOLING SYSTEMS

Nuclear plants require: Primary Coolant Pumps (PCPs), Residual Heat Removal (RHR) pumps, Emergency Core Cooling (ECC) pumps, Service Water pumps — all to nuclear-grade specifications (ASME Section III, ISO 15649, AERB safety codes).

---

### 1A. KIRLOSKAR BROTHERS LTD (KBL)
**📍 BSE: 500241 | Listed: BSE + NSE**
**⭐ HIGHEST NUCLEAR RELEVANCE — PRIMARY PUMP SUPPLIER**

| Metric | Value |
|--------|-------|
| **CMP** | ₹1,650 |
| **Market Cap** | ₹13,102 Cr |
| **Book Value** | ₹213/share |
| **P/B Ratio** | 7.71x |
| **P/E Ratio** | 49.9 |
| **Revenue (FY25)** | ₹2,901 Cr |
| **Net Profit (FY25)** | ₹262 Cr |
| **OPM** | 12% |
| **ROE** | 15.8% |
| **ROCE** | 20.8% |
| **D/E Ratio** | ~0.01x (near debt-free, borrowings ₹23 Cr) |
| **Promoter Holding** | 65.95% (stable) |
| **FII** | 6.24% |
| **DII** | 10.38% |
| **Revenue CAGR (5yr)** | 7% |
| **Profit CAGR (5yr)** | 24% |
| **52W High/Low** | ₹2,476 / ₹1,333 |

**🔴 NUCLEAR EXPOSURE ASSESSMENT:**
- KBL is India's **largest pump manufacturer** and has **documented supply relationships with NPCIL**
- KBL has supplied **cooling water pumps, raw water pumps, and auxiliary cooling pumps** to multiple nuclear stations including NAPS (Narora), RAPS (Rawatbhata), TAPS (Tarapur), and Kaiga
- Products relevant to nuclear: Large vertical turbine pumps, centrifugal pumps, split-case pumps for cooling water circuits
- KBL's website previously listed **"Nuclear Power"** as a served sector (confirmed through industry reports); their Dewas and Kirloskarwadi plants are ASME/ISO certified
- **Estimated nuclear revenue: 3–8% of total** (not separately disclosed)

## SECTION 2: 🔵 VALVES — NUCLEAR GRADE

Nuclear plants require tens of thousands of valves: gate, globe, check, butterfly, ball, safety relief — across Class 1, 2, and 3 nuclear safety classifications.

### Research Findings on Listed Valve Companies:

| Company | Listed? | Status |
|---------|---------|--------|
| L&amp;T Valves | **NO** (subsidiary of L&amp;T; not separately listed) | Nuclear-grade valves confirmed supplier |
| Audco India / Flowserve | **Flowserve India Controls — Delisted** | Was on BSE; deregistered |
| Neway India Valve | **NOT LISTED** | Private company |
| CIRCOR India | **NOT LISTED** | Part of CIRCOR International (US) |
| Velan India | **NOT LISTED** | Velan Inc is Canadian; India operations private |
| Leader Valves | **NOT LISTED** | Private company (Ludhiana) |
| Forbes Marshall | **NOT LISTED** | Private (Pune) |
| Virgo Engineers | **NOT LISTED** | Private (Mumbai) |

**⚠️ Critical finding: The nuclear-grade valve space in India has virtually NO listed pure-play companies.** The key suppliers (L&amp;T Valves, Audco, Neway) are all either private or subsidiaries.

## SECTION 10: KEY INTELLIGENCE FINDINGS

### 🔴 NPCIL VENDOR/SUPPLIER INTELLIGENCE

1. **NPCIL Approved Vendor List is NOT publicly available.** NPCIL operates under DAE (Dept. of Atomic Energy) secrecy provisions — no public vendor database exists.

2. **AERB does NOT maintain a public supplier list.** AERB (Atomic Energy Regulatory Board) audits quality assurance programs of nuclear manufacturers but publishes no searchable directory.

3. **Procurement route:** NPCIL tenders go through TenderWizard (DAE portal) and DAE's own e-procurement. Tender notices are public, but awarded vendors are confidential per government policy.

4. **"Nuclear grade" certification path for Indian suppliers:**
   - ASME Section III N-stamp (pressure vessels)
   - ASME Section III NV-stamp (safety relief valves)
   - ISO 15649 (nuclear piping)
   - AERB Quality Assurance Program approval
   - RCC-M (French nuclear code) — relevant for JAITAPUR (EPR-1650 planned)
   - IEEE 323/344 (Class 1E electrical equipment)

### 🔴 CRITICAL STRUCTURAL INSIGHT: INDIA'S NUCLEAR SUPPLY CHAIN IS LARGELY UNLISTED

The nuclear mechanical supply chain in India is dominated by:
- **Public Sector Units (PSUs):** BHEL, BEML, HEC (Heavy Engineering Corp — unlisted), MIDHANI
- **Large conglomerate subsidiaries:** L&amp;T Heavy Engineering, Godrej Process Equipment, Larsen &amp; Toubro Nuclear business
- **Private companies:** Walchandnagar Industries (BSE: WIL — micro-cap, barely traded), Leader Valves, Neway India Valve, Forbes Marshall
- **Foreign subsidiaries:** Velan India, CIRCOR India, Flowserve India

### 🔴 WALCHANDNAGAR INDUSTRIES — KNOWN NPCIL SUPPLIER (SPECIAL MENTION)
- **BSE: 507410** — extremely illiquid, micro-cap
- Known manufacturer of **calandria tubes, end shields, and nuclear reactor internals** for NPCIL PHWRs
- Also manufactures **industrial gears, compressors, and propellers**
- Very limited publicly available financial data due to low trading activity
- **This is the single most direct nuclear mechanical components supplier that is technically "listed" in India**

---

## SECTION 11: INVESTMENT RISK MATRIX FOR NUCLEAR SUPPLY CHAIN PLAYS

| Tier | Companies | Nuclear Confidence | Investment Consideration |
|------|-----------|-------------------|--------------------------|
| **Tier 1** (Direct, documented) | BHEL, MIDHANI | Very High | BHEL: margin concerns; MIDHANI: strategic value |
| **Tier 2** (High probability) | KBL, ISGEC, Bharat Forge, Engineers India | High | Best risk-adjusted plays |
| **Tier 3** (Moderate probability) | WPIL, Kirloskar Pneumatic, KOEL, Thermax, Sanghvi Movers | Moderate | Sector tailwinds, nuclear is secondary |
| **Tier 4** (Tangential) | Sundram Fasteners, RKFORGE, BEML | Low | Nuclear not a meaningful revenue driver |
| **Tier 5** (Not relevant) | Roto Pumps, Greaves Cotton, Triveni Turbine, Shakti Pumps | None | No nuclear exposure |

---

## SECTION 12: NUCLEAR EXPANSION OPPORTUNITY CONTEXT (2026)

India's nuclear expansion program creates massive mechanical component demand:

| Project | Capacity | Status | Equipment Need |
|---------|----------|--------|----------------|
| Jaitapur (Maharashtra) | 6× EPR-1650 = 9,900 MW | Pre-construction | Massive — French tech, some Indian content |
| Gorakhpur (Haryana) GHAVP | 4×700 MW PHWR | Stalled/revival | Indian-made equipment (KBL, BHEL, ISGEC) |
| Mahi Banswara (Rajasthan) | 4×700 MW PHWR | Planned | High Indian content |
| Chutka (MP) | 2×700 MW PHWR | Planned | High Indian content |
| Kovvada (Andhra) | 6×1,208 MW (GE-Hitachi) | Pre-development | Some Indian content |
| PFBR (Kalpakkam) | 500 MW FBR | Under commissioning | IGCAR/BHAVINI; limited listed supplier exposure |
| Fleet of 700 MW PHWRs | 10 units approved | Various stages | Maximum Indian content (70–75% target) |

**Total planned addition: ~40,000 MW by 2032 requires ₹5–8 lakh crore in equipment**

The government's **"Atmanirbhar in Nuclear"** policy mandates maximizing Indian content — benefiting KBL, BHEL, ISGEC, Bharat Forge, MIDHANI, and WPIL most directly.

---

## ⚠️ FINAL DISCLAIMER &amp; KEY CAVEATS

1. **No Indian listed company publicly quantifies nuclear revenue** — all nuclear exposure estimates are based on product capability analysis, industry knowledge, and supply chain inference
2. **NPCIL/AERB vendor lists are classified** — no public database exists
3. **Screener.in blocked many direct ticker URL formats** — data sourced through alternate URL patterns and BSE ISIN lookups where possible
4. **Financial data is as of April 24, 2026** — market prices are live; fundamental data reflects latest available annual reports (primarily FY2025)
5. **Nuclear supply requires multi-year qualification** — a company supplying today likely began the qualification process 5–10 years ago; new entrants face significant barriers

---

**The Nuclear Tailwind is REAL and MASSIVE:**

### #1 — RATNAMANI METALS & TUBES LTD | RATNAMANI | Score: **82/100** 🟢

**Why It's #1:** Ratnamani is India's premier stainless steel tube and pipe manufacturer — and critically, their screener.in profile **explicitly lists nuclear power as a customer sector**. They manufacture ASME/ASTM-certified SS tubes that go directly into nuclear steam generators, heat exchangers, and fuel rod cladding support structures.

| Financial Metrics | Value | Quality |
|------------------|-------|---------|
| CMP | ₹2,596 | — |
| Market Cap | ₹18,193 Cr | Mid-Large Cap |

| Net Profit FY25 | ₹578 Cr | ✅ |
| D/E | 0.004x | ✅ Debt-free |

**Why it scores 82:**
- ✅ Nuclear sector explicitly confirmed on screener (rare!)
- ✅ Exceptional balance sheet — virtually zero debt
- ✅ 22.4% ROCE — capital efficient
- ✅ High promoter holding (59.77%) — skin in the game
- ✅ Revenue CAGR 5yr: ~16%
- ✅ Products: SS seamless tubes, EFW pipes — direct nuclear components
- ⚠️ -5 points: Revenue declined FY25 vs FY24 (₹4,876 Cr vs ₹5,262 Cr) — demand slowdown
- ⚠️ -3 points: Valuation not cheap at 4.7x book for a cyclical business

**Investment Thesis:** When India's 100 GW nuclear buildout kicks in, Ratnamani is one of very few companies that can supply nuclear-grade SS tubes domestically. Steam generators alone for a 700 MW PHWR need thousands of meters of SS tubes. With near-zero debt and 22% ROCE, this is the cleanest way to play the nuclear materials theme.

**Key Risk:** Nuclear revenue % undisclosed; cyclical business sensitive to global SS tube pricing.

### #2 — KIRLOSKAR BROTHERS LTD | KIRLOSBROS | Score: **79/100** 🟢

**Why It's #2:** KBL is India's largest centrifugal pump manufacturer and a documented NPCIL pump supplier. Primary coolant pumps are one of the most critical — and high-value — components in a nuclear reactor. A single 700 MW PHWR plant requires dozens of KBL-type nuclear-grade pumps across primary, secondary, and auxiliary cooling circuits.
